Use when the user runs /design-sync or says \"sync my design system to Claude Design\".","argumentHint":"[<project hint, e.g. \"Acme DS\">]","builtin":true},{"name":"dataviz","description":"Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. When the destination is a first-party document connector (host-designated, never self-described) that renders live charts, hand it the rows (inline, or as an uploaded data file the chart cites) rather than a rendered PNG/SVG — a picture of a chart loses hover, data inspection and per-value comments. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"update-config","description":"Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"verify","description":"Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by exercising it end-to-end and observing behavior — drive the affected flow, not just tests or typecheck. Run before committing nontrivial changes; bootstraps this repo's project verify skill if none exists yet. Don't invoke it on a diff that only touches tests, docs, or other code with no runtime surface to drive (a change to product source always has one) — there's nothing to observe.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"debug","description":"Enable debug logging for this session and help diagnose issues","argumentHint":"[issue description]","builtin":true},{"name":"code-review","description":"Review the current diff, or a PR number/branch/path target, for correctness bugs (plus reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups where the model's review recipe covers them) at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud); with no level given, it reuses the level you typed last. Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review. For ultra on a GitHub.com PR target, --post asks to post the finished review’s findings to the PR as a single comment from the user’s GitHub account (not a review; the launch dialog still confirms in interactive sessions, while non-interactive mode posts on the flag alone) and --no-post hides that option.","argumentHint":"[low|medium|high|xhigh|max|ultra] [--fix] [--comment] [<pr#>|<branch>|<path>]","aliases":["review"],"builtin":true},{"name":"simplify","description":"Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.","argumentHint":"[<target>]","builtin":true},{"name":"batch","description":"Research and plan a large-scale change, then execute it in parallel across 5–30 isolated worktree agents that each open a PR.","argumentHint":"<instruction>","builtin":true},{"name":"fewer-permission-prompts","description":"Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"doctor","description":"Health-check the user's Claude Code setup and fix issues: diagnose installation health — what the `claude doctor` terminal diagnostics cover — from local data (duplicate or leftover installs, PATH, unparseable settings files, broken or colliding agent definitions, skills whose frontmatter fails to parse); find unused skills, MCP servers, and plugins versus their context cost and disable dead weight; deduplicate local CLAUDE.md files against checked-in ones; trim checked-in CLAUDE.md files by cutting content a session could derive from the codebase (directory layouts, tech-stack lists, architecture overviews) while keeping gotchas, rationale, and non-standard conventions; migrate always-loaded CLAUDE.md guidance into lazy skills and nested CLAUDE.md files; flag slow hooks and context-heavy extensions; check the installed version is current; make auto mode the default permission mode; and pre-approve frequently denied read-only commands. Use when the user asks for a doctor run, checkup, audit, tune-up, or cleanup of their Claude Code setup or configuration.","argumentHint":"","aliases":["checkup"],"builtin":true},{"name":"loop","description":"Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Claude desktop app (also called the in-app browser, the browser pane, Claude's browser, or \"your own browser\"), whose tools are named mcp__Claude_Browser__* when the session runs in the desktop app and mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser tool instead. It covers the pane's persistent sign-ins, tabs and preview_start, reading pages as text, site approvals, what the pane cannot open, and what to do when it cannot be reached. It is not for Claude in Chrome (mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["built-in-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:chrome-browser","description":"Read this skill before the first step that uses Claude in Chrome, the browser extension whose tools are named mcp__claude-in-chrome__* (also called Chrome, the browser extension, or the external browser) and which acts in the person's real Chrome with their own sign-ins; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__claude-in-chrome tool instead. It covers loading the tools in one ToolSearch call, checking the person's open tabs and working in a new tab, site permissions, GIF recordings, console logs, dialogs to avoid, and when to stop and ask. It is not for the built-in browser (mcp__Claude_Browser__* or mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["chrome-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:computer-use","description":"Read this skill before the first step of any request to do something in an app on the person's own computer (Notes, Finder, System Settings, any desktop app), to look at their screen, or for \"computer use\". Computer use (desktop control) lets Claude take screenshots of the person's desktop and control it with clicks, typing and scrolling through the Claude desktop app; its tools are named mcp__computer-use__* when the session runs in the desktop app and mcp__remote-devices__computer_* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before computer use is turned on for a conversation there may be no such tools, only an enable__mcp__remote-devices__computer tool, which turns it on. It covers turning it on, picking the right tool, the access flow, and the safety rules for tiered apps, links and financial actions. It is not for websites, which go through Claude in Chrome or the built-in browser and their own skills. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["computer-use"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:deep-research","description":"Use this skill when the user's prompt requires (1) researching a topic across multiple sources, comparing options or alternatives, analyzing trends or history, understanding markets or industries, or reviewing literature or studies and (2) synthesizing that research into a comprehensive, narrative report. If you're planning to search the web or internal knowledge bases, consider using this skill. This skill coordinates research subagents, so use it only when you have a tool for spawning subagents (the Agent or Task tool); otherwise, research the question directly. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":""},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docs","description":"docs (living docs people share, comment on and edit; use only when the user asks for one: names a doc, document, page, memo, spec, PRD, runbook or write-up, asks for somewhere to share or keep editing something, or says yes to your doc offer; a plan, comparison, summary or notes asked in chat stays in chat (at most a one-line doc offer); a report, status update, recap or \"something I can send them\" with no form named → ask first: reply, doc or file?; tabs hold tables and live charts too; a pasted claude.ai/code/artifact/… link may be a doc: check with docs tools first; not HTML pages, apps or plain chat answers; a .docx/.pptx/.xlsx/PDF asked for by name → that format's skill): asked for one → no docs-connector instructions in context? call the docs connector's `guide` with topic.instructions first, then create the doc (headings only, no body) before any search, file read or plan, even with files attached. Documenting code means docstrings or repo docs, not a doc. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docs"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docx","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx) or Word templates (.dotx). Triggers include: any mention of 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', or requests to produce professional documents with formatting like tables of contents, page numbers, or letterheads. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a 'report', 'memo', 'letter', 'template', or similar deliverable as a Word or .docx file (to download, email or print), use this skill. However, if they ask for a document, page, report, memo, or notes WITHOUT naming a file format and the session offers Claude's own dedicated document or page skill or connector, use that instead. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or coding unrelated to document generation. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:import-memory","description":"Import a memory export from another AI assistant into Claude's memory — conversationally, additively, and with the content treated as data. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["import-memory"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:morning","description":"Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["morning"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pdf","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pdf"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pptx","description":"Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations as PowerPoint (.pptx) files; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email, summary, or creating a different type of slide deck); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user asks for a PowerPoint or .pptx file, or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. However, when the user asks for a deck, slides, a slide deck, or a presentation without naming a file format, default to using a dedicated slide-deck artifact type or a separate slides skill if this session offers one; otherwise, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pptx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:setup-writing-style","description":"Learns how the user writes from their own sent messages and docs, and builds a voice profile so future drafts sound like them instead of generic AI. The profile is saved as the my-writing-style skill. Use when the user asks to set up, learn, or capture their writing voice, or complains that drafts sound generic or unlike them and no my-writing-style profile exists. Only for drafting text the user will send as themselves, not for Claude's own replies. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["setup-writing-style"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:skill-creator","description":"Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["skill-creator"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:xlsx","description":"Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Do NOT trigger when the primary deliverable is a Word document, HTML report, standalone Python script, database pipeline, or Google Sheets API integration, even if tabular data is involved. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["xlsx"]}],"uuid":"8b644ff9-abcb-438b-bf0d-3001f0ba0a2d","session_id":"5a4c4f70-f8bc-4bf7-9998-cc5c20f832fa"}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Cqqmt7pCpKwZFQFYezoGvG","type":"tool_result","content":"25\t} from '@nestjs/common';\n26\timport { ConfigService } from '@nestjs/config';\n27\timport { Reflector } from '@nestjs/core';\n28\timport { JwtService } from '@nestjs/jwt';\n29\timport { toPlatformCapabilities } from '@platform/event-contracts';\n30\timport * as jwt from 'jsonwebtoken';\n31\t\n32\timport { ROLES_KEY } from '../decorators/roles.decorator';\n33\t// APA-371: read the platform-SSoT bypass key (re-exported from backend-common)\n34\t// instead of declaring a local 'isPublic' copy the guard must keep in sync.\n35\timport { IS_PUBLIC_KEY } from '../decorators/public.decorator';\n36\t// Bind the WRITER to the canonical request-user SSoT: AuthenticatedUser extends\n37\t// JwtUser, so `request.user = { ... }` below fails type-check if it omits `sub`\n38\t// (ORPHAN-146). The shared ThrottlerGuard reads that same `sub`.\n39\timport { AuthenticatedRequest } from '../shared/authenticated-request';\n40\t\n41\t// WHY: Explicit @Inject() required — useClass + APP_GUARD relies on design:paramtypes\n42\t// metadata which may not survive all build/runtime environments (Alpine musl, prod-only deps).\n43\t// getJwtVerifyOptions: centralised JWT verification options (RS256 public\n44\t// key, issuer, audience).\n45\t// BEFORE: guard used `import * as jwt from 'jsonwebtoken'` with jwt.verify() —\n46\t// synchronous (blocks event loop), no algorithm restriction, no issuer/audience check.\n47\t// AFTER: guard uses JwtService.verifyAsync() with getJwtVerifyOptions() — async,\n48\t// algorithm-restricted to RS256 (asymmetric — auth-service signs with the\n49\t// private key, every consumer verifies with the public key), issuer + audience\n50\t// enforced at library level. jsonwebtoken still imported for error\n51\t// type-checking in the catch block.\n52\t\n53\t/**\n54\t * JWT payload structure for admin-api guard.\n55\t *\n56\t * SECURITY (H-08): email is now optional because auth-service no longer\n57\t * embeds PII (email, firstName, lastName) in JWT tokens. Services that\n58\t * need the user's email should fetch it from auth-service using the sub (user ID).\n59\t */\n60\texport interface JwtPayload {\n61\t  sub: string;\n62\t  /** @deprecated Optional -- JWT no longer carries email PII (H-08) */\n63\t  email?: string;\n64\t  role?: string;\n65\t  roles?: string[];\n66\t  tenantId?: string;\n67\t  type?: string;\n68\t  jti?: string;\n69\t  iat: number;\n70\t  exp: number /** MFA step-up cleared on this session (auth-service claim). */;\n71\t  mfaVerified?: boolean;\n72\t  /** ADR-0016: platform-operator capabilities minted from auth.platform_capability_grants. */\n73\t  platformCapabilities?: string[];\n74\t}\n75\t\n76\t// Product language calls this actor \"platform admin\"; the auth domain\n77\t// represents that platform-level operator with the existing SUPER_ADMIN role.\n78\tconst DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLES = ['SUPER_ADMIN', 'super_admin'];\n79\t\n80\t/** Extract a human-readable reason from an UnauthorizedException for logging + events. */\n81\tfunction reasonOf(exception: UnauthorizedException): string {\n82\t  const response = exception.getResponse();\n83\t  if (typeof response === 'string') return response;\n84\t  if (response && typeof response === 'object' && 'message' in response) {\n85\t    const message = (response as { message?: unknown }).message;\n86\t    if (typeof message === 'string') return message;\n87\t    if (Array.isArray(message)) return message.join(', ');\n88\t  }\n89\t  return exception.message || 'Unauthorized';\n90\t}\n91\t\n92\t/** Normalize a possibly-array header to a single string value. */\n93\tfunction headerValue(value: string | string[] | undefined): string | undefined {\n94\t  return Array.isArray(value) ? value[0] : value;\n95\t}\n96\t\n97\t@Injectable()\n98\texport class PlatformAdminGuard implements CanActivate {\n99\t  private readonly logger = new Logger(PlatformAdminGuard.name);\n100\t\n101\t  constructor(\n102\t    @Inject(Reflector) private readonly reflector: Reflector,\n103\t    @Inject(ConfigService) private readonly configService: ConfigService,\n104\t    @Inject(JwtService) private readonly jwtService: JwtService,\n105\t    // APA-367: admin-api is a directly-reachable auth boundary (prod nginx routes\n106\t    // /api/ straight here, bypassing gateway-api's blacklist-checking guard), so\n107\t    // it MUST self-enforce token revocation. Both stores are REQUIRED (not\n108\t    // @Optional): the @Global TokenBlacklistModule + UserTokenRevocationModule are\n109\t    // registered in app.module, so a missing wiring fails DI at boot rather than\n110\t    // silently disabling revocation on the most privileged surface. In production\n111\t    // TokenBlacklistService itself fails fast unless Redis is configured, so the\n112\t    // check is cross-instance correct.\n113\t    // TOKEN_BLACKLIST's canonical contract exposes isBlacklisted(). Adapt it to\n114\t    // the shared verifier's read-only isValidToken() capability only at the call\n115\t    // boundary below, so every Nest module resolves the same injection token.\n116\t    @Inject(TOKEN_BLACKLIST)\n117\t    private readonly tokenBlacklist: ITokenBlacklist,\n118\t    @Inject(USER_TOKEN_REVOCATION)\n119\t    private readonly userTokenRevocation: IUserTokenRevocation,\n120\t    // APA-369: this guard is the FIRST APP_GUARD, so a request with a\n121\t    // missing/invalid/expired/forged/revoked Bearer is rejected here BEFORE the\n122\t    // shared ThrottlerGuard (registered second) ever runs — token brute-force /\n123\t    // credential-stuffing against the platform-admin API was never app-throttled\n124\t    // and produced no security event. Reordering the guards is unsound (the\n125\t    // ThrottlerGuard reads request.user.sub, which only THIS guard populates, so\n126\t    // throttle-first would re-classify every authenticated SUPER_ADMIN as\n127\t    // anonymous). Instead we account each auth FAILURE against a per-IP bucket\n128\t    // (only failures increment it, so a valid operator's fan-out never counts)\n129\t    // and emit a security event that reaches the incident pipeline.\n130\t    @Inject(IpRateLimiterService)\n131\t    private readonly failedAuthIpLimiter: IpRateLimiterService,\n132\t    @Inject(SecurityEventService)\n133\t    private readonly securityEvents: SecurityEventService,\n134\t  ) {\n135\t    // SECURITY (CRITICAL-001): JWT_SECRET length validation removed in WS2.B\n136\t    // (2026-04-14). The check was a hold-over from the HS256 era — verifyAsync\n137\t    // already uses getJwtVerifyOptions() which loads the RS256 public key and\n138\t    // throws at startup if it is missing. There is no JWT_SECRET to validate\n139\t    // anymore; reading it would also trip the ESLint no-restricted-syntax\n140\t    // ban on JWT_SECRET reads (WS2.C).\n141\t  }\n142\t\n143\t  async canActivate(context: ExecutionContext): Promise<boolean> {\n144\t    // Hybrid-app boundary: this guard authenticates the HTTP surface only.\n145\t    // admin-api-service also consumes NATS events (RPC context) — those are\n146\t    // authenticated by the broker-verified mTLS client-cert CN (ADR-015) and\n147\t    // the per-subject publish ACL (e.g. only farm-service may publish\n148\t    // TenantOnboardingAck), NOT by a Bearer JWT, which does not exist on the\n149\t    // NATS surface. Without this short-circuit the guard would call\n150\t    // switchToHttp().getRequest() on an RPC context and reject every event.\n151\t    if (context.getType() !== 'http') {\n152\t      return true;\n153\t    }\n154\t\n155\t    // Check if route is marked as public\n156\t    const isPublic = this.reflector.getAllAndOverride<boolean>(IS_PUBLIC_KEY, [\n157\t      context.getHandler(),\n158\t      context.getClass(),\n159\t    ]);\n160\t\n161\t    if (isPublic) {\n162\t      return true;\n163\t    }\n164\t\n165\t    const request = context.switchToHttp().getRequest<AuthenticatedRequest>();\n166\t    const authHeader = request.headers.authorization;\n167\t\n168\t    if (!authHeader) {\n169\t      return this.rejectAuth(\n170\t        context,\n171\t        request,\n172\t        new UnauthorizedException('No authorization header provided'),\n173\t      );\n174\t    }"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"5a4c4f70-f8bc-4bf7-9998-cc5c20f832fa","uuid":"86ae1b39-eb24-4c70-bc1d-593c7e344346","timestamp":"2026-09-19T15:24:00.980Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-a4a4db32a408/apps/admin-api-service/src/guards/platform-admin.guard.ts","content":"} from '@nestjs/common';\nimport { ConfigService } from '@nestjs/config';\nimport { Reflector } from '@nestjs/core';\nimport { JwtService } from '@nestjs/jwt';\nimport { toPlatformCapabilities } from '@platform/event-contracts';\nimport * as jwt from 'jsonwebtoken';\n\nimport { ROLES_KEY } from '../decorators/roles.decorator';\n// APA-371: read the platform-SSoT bypass key (re-exported from backend-common)\n// instead of declaring a local 'isPublic' copy the guard must keep in sync.\nimport { IS_PUBLIC_KEY } from '../decorators/public.decorator';\n// Bind the WRITER to the canonical request-user SSoT: AuthenticatedUser extends\n// JwtUser, so `request.user = { ... }` below fails type-check if it omits `sub`\n// (ORPHAN-146). The shared ThrottlerGuard reads that same `sub`.\nimport { AuthenticatedRequest } from '../shared/authenticated-request';\n\n// WHY: Explicit @Inject() required — useClass + APP_GUARD relies on design:paramtypes\n// metadata which may not survive all build/runtime environments (Alpine musl, prod-only deps).\n// getJwtVerifyOptions: centralised JWT verification options (RS256 public\n// key, issuer, audience).\n// BEFORE: guard used `import * as jwt from 'jsonwebtoken'` with jwt.verify() —\n// synchronous (blocks event loop), no algorithm restriction, no issuer/audience check.\n// AFTER: guard uses JwtService.verifyAsync() with getJwtVerifyOptions() — async,\n// algorithm-restricted to RS256 (asymmetric — auth-service signs with the\n// private key, every consumer verifies with the public key), issuer + audience\n// enforced at library level. jsonwebtoken still imported for error\n// type-checking in the catch block.\n\n/**\n * JWT payload structure for admin-api guard.\n *\n * SECURITY (H-08): email is now optional because auth-service no longer\n * embeds PII (email, firstName, lastName) in JWT tokens. Services that\n * need the user's email should fetch it from auth-service using the sub (user ID).\n */\nexport interface JwtPayload {\n  sub: string;\n  /** @deprecated Optional -- JWT no longer carries email PII (H-08) */\n  email?: string;\n  role?: string;\n  roles?: string[];\n  tenantId?: string;\n  type?: string;\n  jti?: string;\n  iat: number;\n  exp: number /** MFA step-up cleared on this session (auth-service claim). */;\n  mfaVerified?: boolean;\n  /** ADR-0016: platform-operator capabilities minted from auth.platform_capability_grants. */\n  platformCapabilities?: string[];\n}\n\n// Product language calls this actor \"platform admin\"; the auth domain\n// represents that platform-level operator with the existing SUPER_ADMIN role.\nconst DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLES = ['SUPER_ADMIN', 'super_admin'];\n\n/** Extract a human-readable reason from an UnauthorizedException for logging + events. */\nfunction reasonOf(exception: UnauthorizedException): string {\n  const response = exception.getResponse();\n  if (typeof response === 'string') return response;\n  if (response && typeof response === 'object' && 'message' in response) {\n    const message = (response as { message?: unknown }).message;\n    if (typeof message === 'string') return message;\n    if (Array.isArray(message)) return message.join(', ');\n  }\n  return exception.message || 'Unauthorized';\n}\n\n/** Normalize a possibly-array header to a single string value. */\nfunction headerValue(value: string | string[] | undefined): string | undefined {\n  return Array.isArray(value) ? value[0] : value;\n}\n\n@Injectable()\nexport class PlatformAdminGuard implements CanActivate {\n  private readonly logger = new Logger(PlatformAdminGuard.name);\n\n  constructor(\n    @Inject(Reflector) private readonly reflector: Reflector,\n    @Inject(ConfigService) private readonly configService: ConfigService,\n    @Inject(JwtService) private readonly jwtService: JwtService,\n    // APA-367: admin-api is a directly-reachable auth boundary (prod nginx routes\n    // /api/ straight here, bypassing gateway-api's blacklist-checking guard), so\n    // it MUST self-enforce token revocation. Both stores are REQUIRED (not\n    // @Optional): the @Global TokenBlacklistModule + UserTokenRevocationModule are\n    // registered in app.module, so a missing wiring fails DI at boot rather than\n    // silently disabling revocation on the most privileged surface. In production\n    // TokenBlacklistService itself fails fast unless Redis is configured, so the\n    // check is cross-instance correct.\n    // TOKEN_BLACKLIST's canonical contract exposes isBlacklisted(). Adapt it to\n    // the shared verifier's read-only isValidToken() capability only at the call\n    // boundary below, so every Nest module resolves the same injection token.\n    @Inject(TOKEN_BLACKLIST)\n    private readonly tokenBlacklist: ITokenBlacklist,\n    @Inject(USER_TOKEN_REVOCATION)\n    private readonly userTokenRevocation: IUserTokenRevocation,\n    // APA-369: this guard is the FIRST APP_GUARD, so a request with a\n    // missing/invalid/expired/forged/revoked Bearer is rejected here BEFORE the\n    // shared ThrottlerGuard (registered second) ever runs — token brute-force /\n    // credential-stuffing against the platform-admin API was never app-throttled\n    // and produced no security event. Reordering the guards is unsound (the\n    // ThrottlerGuard reads request.user.sub, which only THIS guard populates, so\n    // throttle-first would re-classify every authenticated SUPER_ADMIN as\n    // anonymous). Instead we account each auth FAILURE against a per-IP bucket\n    // (only failures increment it, so a valid operator's fan-out never counts)\n    // and emit a security event that reaches the incident pipeline.\n    @Inject(IpRateLimiterService)\n    private readonly failedAuthIpLimiter: IpRateLimiterService,\n    @Inject(SecurityEventService)\n    private readonly securityEvents: SecurityEventService,\n  ) {\n    // SECURITY (CRITICAL-001): JWT_SECRET length validation removed in WS2.B\n    // (2026-04-14). The check was a hold-over from the HS256 era — verifyAsync\n    // already uses getJwtVerifyOptions() which loads the RS256 public key and\n    // throws at startup if it is missing. There is no JWT_SECRET to validate\n    // anymore; reading it would also trip the ESLint no-restricted-syntax\n    // ban on JWT_SECRET reads (WS2.C).\n  }\n\n  async canActivate(context: ExecutionContext): Promise<boolean> {\n    // Hybrid-app boundary: this guard authenticates the HTTP surface only.\n    // admin-api-service also consumes NATS events (RPC context) — those are\n    // authenticated by the broker-verified mTLS client-cert CN (ADR-015) and\n    // the per-subject publish ACL (e.g. only farm-service may publish\n    // TenantOnboardingAck), NOT by a Bearer JWT, which does not exist on the\n    // NATS surface. Without this short-circuit the guard would call\n    // switchToHttp().getRequest() on an RPC context and reject every event.\n    if (context.getType() !== 'http') {\n      return true;\n    }\n\n    // Check if route is marked as public\n    const isPublic = this.reflector.getAllAndOverride<boolean>(IS_PUBLIC_KEY, [\n      context.getHandler(),\n      context.getClass(),\n    ]);\n\n    if (isPublic) {\n      return true;\n    }\n\n    const request = context.switchToHttp().getRequest<AuthenticatedRequest>();\n    const authHeader = request.headers.authorization;\n\n    if (!authHeader) {\n      return this.rejectAuth(\n        context,\n        request,\n        new UnauthorizedException('No authorization header provided'),\n      );\n    }","numLines":150,"startLine":25,"totalLines":382}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01LtepL2NpTeLZ34oRpGfgg5","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t/**\n2\t * Platform-wide invariant — SEC-CRITICAL-001 / SECREV-CRITICAL-001:\n3\t *\n4\t * Every signer of internal-service HTTP traffic MUST go through one of\n5\t * the two canonical helpers in `libs/backend-common/src/utils/service-identity.util.ts`:\n6\t *\n7\t *   - `generateServiceIdentityHeadersV2`  (preferred — full method/path/body bind)\n8\t *   - `generateServiceIdentityHeaders`    (deprecated v1 — kept for one release)\n9\t *\n10\t * Hand-rolled HMAC over the v1 canonical (`timestamp:service:tenantId`)\n11\t * outside this util re-introduces the regression class the audit\n12\t * captured: cross-endpoint replay + body-tampering on inter-service calls.\n13\t *\n14\t * # What this test enforces\n15\t *\n16\t *   1. The two generator exports exist.\n17\t *   2. The unified verifier export `verifyServiceIdentityRequest` exists\n18\t *      (single canonical entry point for receivers).\n19\t *   3. No NEW grep hit appears matching the v1 canonical-string shape\n20\t *      `${timestamp}:${serviceName}:${tenantId}` outside the canonical\n21\t *      util. A small allowlist captures the legitimate hand-rolled HMAC\n22\t *      paths that exist for unrelated protocols (TOTP, password reset,\n23\t *      Stripe webhook) — each entry is justified inline.\n24\t *\n25\t * # Why source-level invariant\n26\t *\n27\t * The receiver guard is ONE place; the generator helpers are TWO. A\n28\t * source-level grep is the cheapest reliable check. Catches:\n29\t *   - copy-pasted v1 canonical string into a new caller\n30\t *   - someone re-introducing the deprecated shape after the W0.A-finalize\n31\t *     removal\n32\t *   - a fresh agent generating signature code from training data instead\n33\t *     of using the canonical helper\n34\t */\n35\t\n36\timport { execSync } from 'node:child_process';\n37\timport { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';\n38\timport { resolve } from 'node:path';\n39\t\n40\tconst REPO_ROOT = resolve(__dirname, '..', '..');\n41\tconst UTIL_PATH = 'libs/backend-common/src/utils/service-identity.util.ts';\n42\t\n43\t/**\n44\t * Files that legitimately use HMAC-SHA256 for protocols OTHER than\n45\t * service-identity. Each entry is allowed to contain `createHmac` calls\n46\t * but is asserted NOT to use the v1 service-identity canonical string\n47\t * shape `${timestamp}:${serviceName}:${tenantId}`.\n48\t */\n49\tconst HMAC_ALLOWLIST: ReadonlyArray<{ path: string; reason: string }> = [\n50\t  {\n51\t    path: 'libs/backend-common/src/utils/hmac-tenant-hash.util.ts',\n52\t    reason: 'Tenant-schema HMAC for log redaction. Different protocol, no service-identity overlap.',\n53\t  },\n54\t  {\n55\t    path: 'libs/backend-common/src/auth/password.util.ts',\n56\t    reason: 'Password pepper HMAC for credential storage. Different protocol.',\n57\t  },\n58\t  {\n59\t    path: 'apps/billing-service/src/billing/controllers/stripe-webhook.controller.ts',\n60\t    reason: 'Stripe webhook signature verification — Stripe-defined protocol, distinct from internal service identity.',\n61\t  },\n62\t  {\n63\t    path: 'apps/admin-api-service/src/auth/password-reset.controller.ts',\n64\t    reason: 'Password-reset token HMAC. Different protocol, no inter-service exposure.',\n65\t  },\n66\t  {\n67\t    path: 'apps/auth-service/src/modules/authentication/services/mfa.service.ts',\n68\t    reason: 'TOTP RFC 6238 HMAC-SHA1 for one-time codes. Different protocol.',\n69\t  },\n70\t  {\n71\t    path: 'apps/gateway-api/src/guards/strategies/api-key-auth.strategy.ts',\n72\t    reason: 'External API-key HMAC for tenant-issued API keys. Different protocol from internal-service identity.',\n73\t  },\n74\t  {\n75\t    path: 'libs/backend-common/src/middleware/strip-internal-headers.middleware.ts',\n76\t    reason: 'Strip-vs-trust gate using HMAC(serviceName, secret) only. Narrower contract — proof-of-secret-possession; not a SEC-CRITICAL-001 surface. Promoted to backend-common in W0.I.',\n77\t  },\n78\t];\n79\t\n80\tdescribe('INVARIANT (SEC-CRITICAL-001): service-identity canonical coverage', () => {\n81\t  it('exports both v1 (deprecated) and v2 generators from the canonical util', () => {\n82\t    const src = readFileSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT, UTIL_PATH), 'utf8');\n83\t    expect(src).toMatch(/export function generateServiceIdentityHeadersV2/);\n84\t    expect(src).toMatch(/export function generateServiceIdentityHeaders\\b/);\n85\t    expect(src).toMatch(/export function verifyServiceIdentityRequest\\b/);\n86\t    expect(src).toMatch(/export function verifyServiceIdentityV2\\b/);\n87\t    expect(src).toMatch(/export function verifyServiceIdentity\\b/);\n88\t  });\n89\t\n90\t  it('the v2 canonical input binds method, path, and body-hash', () => {\n91\t    const src = readFileSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT, UTIL_PATH), 'utf8');\n92\t    // The function buildCanonicalV2 is internal but its body must list the\n93\t    // four bound fields for the regression check to be visible.\n94\t    expect(src).toMatch(/buildCanonicalV2/);\n95\t    expect(src).toMatch(/SIG_VERSION_V2/);\n96\t    // Sanity — the v2 header set includes the four extra headers.\n97\t    expect(src).toMatch(/'X-Service-Method'/);\n98\t    expect(src).toMatch(/'X-Service-Path'/);\n99\t    expect(src).toMatch(/'X-Service-Body-Hash'/);\n100\t    expect(src).toMatch(/'X-Service-Sig-Version'/);\n101\t  });\n102\t\n103\t  it('no production code (outside the canonical util + allowlist) constructs the v1 canonical string shape', () => {\n104\t    // Pattern: any literal containing \"${timestamp}:${serviceName}:${tenantId}\"\n105\t    // — the exact v1 input. A tamperer reintroducing this shape would\n106\t    // emit it in a template literal exactly like the deprecated function.\n107\t    let grepOut: string;\n108\t    try {\n109\t      grepOut = execSync(\n110\t        `git -C ${REPO_ROOT} grep -lE '\\\\$\\\\{timestamp\\\\}:\\\\$\\\\{serviceName\\\\}:\\\\$\\\\{tenantId\\\\}' -- 'apps/**/*.ts' 'libs/**/*.ts' 'platform/**/*.ts' ':!**/__tests__/**' ':!**/*.spec.ts' ':!**/node_modules/**'`,\n111\t        { encoding: 'utf8' },\n112\t      );\n113\t    } catch (err) {\n114\t      const e = err as { status?: number };\n115\t      if (e.status === 1) {\n116\t        // No matches — the canonical util uses a different shape internally\n117\t        // (separated by newline + version prefix), so even it doesn't match.\n118\t        return;\n119\t      }\n120\t      throw err;\n121\t    }\n122\t\n123\t    const offending = grepOut\n124\t      .trim()\n125\t      .split('\\n')\n126\t      .filter(Boolean)\n127\t      .filter((p) => !p.endsWith(UTIL_PATH));\n128\t\n129\t    if (offending.length > 0) {\n130\t      throw new Error(\n131\t        'New code uses the deprecated v1 service-identity canonical input ' +\n132\t          '(`${timestamp}:${serviceName}:${tenantId}`). Use generateServiceIdentityHeadersV2 ' +\n133\t          'or signedFetch from libs/backend-common instead.\\nOffending files:\\n  - ' +\n134\t          offending.join('\\n  - '),\n135\t      );\n136\t    }\n137\t  });\n138\t\n139\t  it('the HMAC allowlist documents every legitimate non-service-identity HMAC user', () => {\n140\t    // Every allowlist entry must reference a real file. Stale allowlist"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"5a4c4f70-f8bc-4bf7-9998-cc5c20f832fa","uuid":"736003d9-bca6-401f-80ae-eddae1ea9d79","timestamp":"2026-09-19T15:24:19.054Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-a4a4db32a408/tests/invariants/service-identity-canonical-coverage.spec.ts","content":"/**\n * Platform-wide invariant — SEC-CRITICAL-001 / SECREV-CRITICAL-001:\n *\n * Every signer of internal-service HTTP traffic MUST go through one of\n * the two canonical helpers in `libs/backend-common/src/utils/service-identity.util.ts`:\n *\n *   - `generateServiceIdentityHeadersV2`  (preferred — full method/path/body bind)\n *   - `generateServiceIdentityHeaders`    (deprecated v1 — kept for one release)\n *\n * Hand-rolled HMAC over the v1 canonical (`timestamp:service:tenantId`)\n * outside this util re-introduces the regression class the audit\n * captured: cross-endpoint replay + body-tampering on inter-service calls.\n *\n * # What this test enforces\n *\n *   1. The two generator exports exist.\n *   2. The unified verifier export `verifyServiceIdentityRequest` exists\n *      (single canonical entry point for receivers).\n *   3. No NEW grep hit appears matching the v1 canonical-string shape\n *      `${timestamp}:${serviceName}:${tenantId}` outside the canonical\n *      util. A small allowlist captures the legitimate hand-rolled HMAC\n *      paths that exist for unrelated protocols (TOTP, password reset,\n *      Stripe webhook) — each entry is justified inline.\n *\n * # Why source-level invariant\n *\n * The receiver guard is ONE place; the generator helpers are TWO. A\n * source-level grep is the cheapest reliable check. Catches:\n *   - copy-pasted v1 canonical string into a new caller\n *   - someone re-introducing the deprecated shape after the W0.A-finalize\n *     removal\n *   - a fresh agent generating signature code from training data instead\n *     of using the canonical helper\n */\n\nimport { execSync } from 'node:child_process';\nimport { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';\nimport { resolve } from 'node:path';\n\nconst REPO_ROOT = resolve(__dirname, '..', '..');\nconst UTIL_PATH = 'libs/backend-common/src/utils/service-identity.util.ts';\n\n/**\n * Files that legitimately use HMAC-SHA256 for protocols OTHER than\n * service-identity. Each entry is allowed to contain `createHmac` calls\n * but is asserted NOT to use the v1 service-identity canonical string\n * shape `${timestamp}:${serviceName}:${tenantId}`.\n */\nconst HMAC_ALLOWLIST: ReadonlyArray<{ path: string; reason: string }> = [\n  {\n    path: 'libs/backend-common/src/utils/hmac-tenant-hash.util.ts',\n    reason: 'Tenant-schema HMAC for log redaction. Different protocol, no service-identity overlap.',\n  },\n  {\n    path: 'libs/backend-common/src/auth/password.util.ts',\n    reason: 'Password pepper HMAC for credential storage. Different protocol.',\n  },\n  {\n    path: 'apps/billing-service/src/billing/controllers/stripe-webhook.controller.ts',\n    reason: 'Stripe webhook signature verification — Stripe-defined protocol, distinct from internal service identity.',\n  },\n  {\n    path: 'apps/admin-api-service/src/auth/password-reset.controller.ts',\n    reason: 'Password-reset token HMAC. Different protocol, no inter-service exposure.',\n  },\n  {\n    path: 'apps/auth-service/src/modules/authentication/services/mfa.service.ts',\n    reason: 'TOTP RFC 6238 HMAC-SHA1 for one-time codes. Different protocol.',\n  },\n  {\n    path: 'apps/gateway-api/src/guards/strategies/api-key-auth.strategy.ts',\n    reason: 'External API-key HMAC for tenant-issued API keys. Different protocol from internal-service identity.',\n  },\n  {\n    path: 'libs/backend-common/src/middleware/strip-internal-headers.middleware.ts',\n    reason: 'Strip-vs-trust gate using HMAC(serviceName, secret) only. Narrower contract — proof-of-secret-possession; not a SEC-CRITICAL-001 surface. Promoted to backend-common in W0.I.',\n  },\n];\n\ndescribe('INVARIANT (SEC-CRITICAL-001): service-identity canonical coverage', () => {\n  it('exports both v1 (deprecated) and v2 generators from the canonical util', () => {\n    const src = readFileSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT, UTIL_PATH), 'utf8');\n    expect(src).toMatch(/export function generateServiceIdentityHeadersV2/);\n    expect(src).toMatch(/export function generateServiceIdentityHeaders\\b/);\n    expect(src).toMatch(/export function verifyServiceIdentityRequest\\b/);\n    expect(src).toMatch(/export function verifyServiceIdentityV2\\b/);\n    expect(src).toMatch(/export function verifyServiceIdentity\\b/);\n  });\n\n  it('the v2 canonical input binds method, path, and body-hash', () => {\n    const src = readFileSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT, UTIL_PATH), 'utf8');\n    // The function buildCanonicalV2 is internal but its body must list the\n    // four bound fields for the regression check to be visible.\n    expect(src).toMatch(/buildCanonicalV2/);\n    expect(src).toMatch(/SIG_VERSION_V2/);\n    // Sanity — the v2 header set includes the four extra headers.\n    expect(src).toMatch(/'X-Service-Method'/);\n    expect(src).toMatch(/'X-Service-Path'/);\n    expect(src).toMatch(/'X-Service-Body-Hash'/);\n    expect(src).toMatch(/'X-Service-Sig-Version'/);\n  });\n\n  it('no production code (outside the canonical util + allowlist) constructs the v1 canonical string shape', () => {\n    // Pattern: any literal containing \"${timestamp}:${serviceName}:${tenantId}\"\n    // — the exact v1 input. A tamperer reintroducing this shape would\n    // emit it in a template literal exactly like the deprecated function.\n    let grepOut: string;\n    try {\n      grepOut = execSync(\n        `git -C ${REPO_ROOT} grep -lE '\\\\$\\\\{timestamp\\\\}:\\\\$\\\\{serviceName\\\\}:\\\\$\\\\{tenantId\\\\}' -- 'apps/**/*.ts' 'libs/**/*.ts' 'platform/**/*.ts' ':!**/__tests__/**' ':!**/*.spec.ts' ':!**/node_modules/**'`,\n        { encoding: 'utf8' },\n      );\n    } catch (err) {\n      const e = err as { status?: number };\n      if (e.status === 1) {\n        // No matches — the canonical util uses a different shape internally\n        // (separated by newline + version prefix), so even it doesn't match.\n        return;\n      }\n      throw err;\n    }\n\n    const offending = grepOut\n      .trim()\n      .split('\\n')\n      .filter(Boolean)\n      .filter((p) => !p.endsWith(UTIL_PATH));\n\n    if (offending.length > 0) {\n      throw new Error(\n        'New code uses the deprecated v1 service-identity canonical input ' +\n          '(`${timestamp}:${serviceName}:${tenantId}`). Use generateServiceIdentityHeadersV2 ' +\n          'or signedFetch from libs/backend-common instead.\\nOffending files:\\n  - ' +\n          offending.join('\\n  - '),\n      );\n    }\n  });\n\n  it('the HMAC allowlist documents every legitimate non-service-identity HMAC user', () => {\n    // Every allowlist entry must reference a real file. Public (unauthenticated)\n16\t *      handlers are exempt: a password-reset by token has no principal.\n17\t *   3. line.replace(/(?<!:)\\/\\/.*$/, ''))\n45\t    .join('\\n');\n46\t}\n47\t\n48\tfunction gitFiles(pathspecs: string[]): string[] {\n49\t  return execFileSync(\n50\t    'git',\n51\t    ['-C', REPO_ROOT, 'ls-files', '--cached', '--others', '--exclude-standard', '--', ...pathspecs],\n52\t    {\n53\t      encoding: 'utf8',\n54\t    },\n55\t  )\n56\t    .split('\\n')\n57\t    .filter(Boolean);\n58\t}\n59\t\n60\tfunction gitGrepFiles(args: string[]): string[] {\n61\t  try {\n62\t    return execFileSync('git', ['-C', REPO_ROOT, 'grep', '-l', '--untracked', ...args], {\n63\t throw err;\n70\t  }\n71\t}\n72\t\n73\tconst IRREVERSIBLE_NAME =\n74\t  /(erase|archive|purge|drop|release|export|terminate|revoke|rollback|deprovision)/i;\n75\t\n76\tdescribe('INVARIANT (ADR-0011): );\n93\t  });\n94\t\n95\t  it('every irreversible admin handler carries @Destructive(), which installs its own guard', () => {\n96\t    const decorator = stripComments(\n97\t handler.isPublic) continue;\n104\t      if (!/@Destructive\\(/.test(handler.block)) unguarded.push(handler.id);\n105\t    }\n106\t    expect(unguarded).toEqual([]);\n107\t  });\n108\t\n109\t  it('the * INVARIANT — platform-admin MFA has one switch and one mechanism\n * (ADR-0011, SEC-CRITICAL-058).\n *\n * The mechanism ships now; enforcement is dated. What this spec keeps true:\n *\n *   1. auth-service's TokenService refuses a SUPER_ADMIN token for an\n *      un-enrolled account once the switch has passed, and names the account\n *      in a security event before then — asserted as unit behaviour in the\n *      service's own spec, whose test titles are pinned here so the behaviour\n *      cannot be deleted with its test.\n *   2. Every irreversible admin handler — a DELETE, or a handler named\n *      erase / archive / purge / drop / release / export / terminate /\n *      revoke / rollback / deprovision — carries @Destructive(), which\n *      installs DestructiveActionGuard itself. Public (unauthenticated)\n *      handlers are exempt: a password-reset by token has no principal.\n *   3. The switch is the only switch: no committed compose or env file sets\n *      MFA_REQUIRED_FOR_CROSS_TENANT=false, and the auth-service compose\n *      entry states SUPER_ADMIN_MFA_ENFORCED_AT as a literal that parses.\n *   4. `security.mfa_enabled` has no reader anywhere: an operator toggle for\n *      a mandatory control is gone.\n */\n\nimport { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';\nimport { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';\nimport { resolve } from 'node:path';\n\nimport * as yaml from 'js-yaml';\n\nimport { parsePlatformAdminMfaPolicy } from '../../libs/backend-common/src/security/platform-admin-mfa-policy';\n\nimport { allAdminMutationHandlers } from './lib/admin-mutation-handlers';\n\nconst REPO_ROOT = resolve(__dirname, '..', '..');\n\nfunction read(rel: string): string {\n  return readFileSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT, rel), 'utf8');\n}\n\nfunction stripComments(src: string): string {\n  return src\n    .replace(/\\/\\*[\\s\\S]*?\\*\\//g, '')\n    .split('\\n')\n    .map((line) => line.replace(/(?<!:)\\/\\/.*$/, ''))\n    .join('\\n');\n}\n\nfunction gitFiles(pathspecs: string[]): string[] {\n  return execFileSync(\n    'git',\n    ['-C', REPO_ROOT, 'ls-files', '--cached', '--others', '--exclude-standard', '--', ...pathspecs],\n    {\n      encoding: 'utf8',\n    },\n  )\n    .split('\\n')\n    .filter(Boolean);\n}\n\nfunction gitGrepFiles(args: string[]): string[] {\n  try {\n    return execFileSync('git', ['-C', REPO_ROOT, 'grep', '-l', '--untracked', ...args], {\n      encoding: 'utf8',\n    })\n      .split('\\n')\n      .filter(Boolean);\n  } catch (err: unknown) {\n    if ((err as { status?: number }).status === 1) return [];\n    throw err;\n  }\n}\n\nconst IRREVERSIBLE_NAME =\n  /(erase|archive|purge|drop|release|export|terminate|revoke|rollback|deprovision)/i;\n\ndescribe('INVARIANT (ADR-0011): platform-admin MFA — one switch, one mechanism', () => {\n  it('auth-service refuses or records an un-enrolled SUPER_ADMIN at token issue, and its spec proves both', () => {\n    const service = stripComments(\n      read('apps/auth-service/src/modules/authentication/services/token.service.ts'),\n    );\n    expect(service).toMatch(/user\\.role === Role\\.SUPER_ADMIN && !user\\.mfaEnabled/);\n    expect(service).toMatch(/readPlatformAdminMfaPolicy\\(\\)/);\n    expect(service).toMatch(/if \\(policy\\.enforced\\) \\{\\s*throw new ForbiddenException/);\n    const spec = read(\n      'apps/auth-service/src/modules/authentication/services/token.service.spec.ts',\n    );\n    expect(spec).toContain(\n      'refuses a SUPER_ADMIN token for an un-enrolled account once enforcement has started',\n    );\n    expect(spec).toContain(\n      'records an un-enrolled SUPER_ADMIN in detective mode and still mints the token',\n    );\n  });\n\n  it('every irreversible admin handler carries @Destructive(), which installs its own guard', () => {\n    const decorator = stripComments(\n      read('libs/backend-common/src/decorators/destructive.decorator.ts'),\n    );\n    expect(decorator).toMatch(/UseGuards\\(DestructiveActionGuard\\)/);\n    const unguarded: string[] = [];\n    for (const handler of allAdminMutationHandlers()) {\n      const irreversible = handler.verb === 'Delete' || IRREVERSIBLE_NAME.test(handler.name);\n      if (!irreversible || handler.isPublic) continue;\n      if (!/@Destructive\\(/.test(handler.block)) unguarded.push(handler.id);\n    }\n    expect(unguarded).toEqual([]);\n  });\n\n  it('the destructive-event sink is bound globally in admin-api so detective mode leaves a row', () => {\n    const auditModule = stripComments(read('apps/admin-api-service/src/audit/audit.module.ts'));\n    expect(auditModule).toMatch(/provide: DESTRUCTIVE_EVENT_SINK/);\n    expect(auditModule).toMatch(/@Global\\(\\)/);\n    expect(auditModule).toMatch(/exports: \\[[^\\]]*DESTRUCTIVE_EVENT_SINK/);\n  });\n\n  it('no committed compose or env file switches cross-tenant MFA off', () => {\n    const offenders = gitGrepFiles([\n      '-E',\n      '-e',\n      \"MFA_REQUIRED_FOR_CROSS_TENANT[=:]\\\\s*'?false'?\",","numLines":120,"startLine":1,"totalLines":155}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01CjoRrghFToaw6a55Jvuk4d","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t/**\n2\t * Password Reset Controller - Security Tests\n3\t *\n4\t * Tests cover:\n5\t * - DTO validation (email, password)\n6\t * - Email enumeration prevention\n7\t * - Auth-service delegation for reset-token/password state\n8\t * - ThrottlePasswordReset decorator presence\n9\t * - Public decorator presence (bypass auth for these endpoints)\n10\t */\n11\timport { INestApplication, ValidationPipe, HttpStatus } from '@nestjs/common';\n12\timport { Test, TestingModule } from '@nestjs/testing';\n13\timport { AUTH_PUBLIC_COMMAND_SUBJECTS } from '@platform/event-contracts';\n14\timport { of, throwError } from 'rxjs';\n15\timport request from 'supertest';\n16\t\n17\timport { PasswordResetController } from '../password-reset.controller';\n18\t\n19\tdescribe('PasswordResetController Security', () => {\n20\t  let app: INestApplication;\n21\t\n22\t  type TestApp = Parameters<typeof request>[0];\n23\t  type PasswordResetResponseBody = {\n24\t    success?: boolean;\n25\t    message?: string;\n26\t  };\n27\t  type AuthNatsSend = jest.Mock<unknown, [string, unknown]>;\n28\t\n29\t  const mockAuthNatsClient = {\n30\t    send: jest.fn() as AuthNatsSend,\n31\t  };\n32\t\n33\t  function httpServer(): TestApp {\n34\t    return app.getHttpServer() as TestApp;\n35\t  }\n36\t\n37\t  function responseBody(res: request.Response): PasswordResetResponseBody {\n38\t    return res.body as PasswordResetResponseBody;\n39\t  }\n40\t\n41\t  function isRecord(value: unknown): value is Record<string, unknown> {\n42\t    return typeof value === 'object' && value !== null && !Array.isArray(value);\n43\t  }\n44\t\n45\t  function authCommandAt(index: number): Record<string, unknown> {\n46\t    const call = mockAuthNatsClient.send.mock.calls[index];\n47\t    if (!call) {\n48\t      throw new Error(`Expected auth NATS command at call index ${index}`);\n49\t    }\n50\t    const payload = call[1];\n51\t    if (!isRecord(payload)) {\n52\t      throw new Error(`Expected auth NATS command payload at call index ${index}`);\n53\t    }\n54\t    return payload;\n55\t  }\n56\t\n57\t  beforeAll(async () => {\n58\t    const module: TestingModule = await Test.createTestingModule({\n59\t      controllers: [PasswordResetController],\n60\t      providers: [\n61\t        { provide: 'AUTH_NATS_CLIENT', useValue: mockAuthNatsClient },\n62\t      ],\n63\t    }).compile();\n64\t\n65\t    app = module.createNestApplication();\n66\t    app.useGlobalPipes(\n67\t      new ValidationPipe({\n68\t        whitelist: true,\n69\t        forbidNonWhitelisted: true,\n70\t        transform: true,\n71\t      }),\n72\t    );\n73\t    await app.init();\n74\t  });\n75\t\n76\t  afterAll(async () => {\n77\t    await app.close();\n78\t  });\n79\t\n80\t  beforeEach(() => {\n81\t    jest.clearAllMocks();\n82\t    mockAuthNatsClient.send.mockReturnValue(of({ success: true }));\n83\t  });\n84\t\n85\t  // ========================================================================\n86\t  // 1. Forgot Password - Validation\n87\t  // ========================================================================\n88\t  describe('POST /auth/forgot-password - Validation', () => {\n89\t    it('should accept valid email', async () => {\n90\t      const res = await request(httpServer())\n91\t        .post('/auth/forgot-password')\n92\t        .send({ email: 'user@example.com' });\n93\t\n94\t      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.OK);\n95\t      expect(responseBody(res).success).toBe(true);\n96\t    });\n97\t\n98\t    it('should reject missing email', async () => {\n99\t      const res = await request(httpServer())\n100\t        .post('/auth/forgot-password')\n101\t        .send({});\n102\t\n103\t      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST);\n104\t    });\n105\t\n106\t    it('should reject invalid email format', async () => {\n107\t      const res = await request(httpServer())\n108\t        .post('/auth/forgot-password')\n109\t        .send({ email: 'not-an-email' });\n110\t\n111\t      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST);\n112\t    });\n113\t\n114\t    it('should reject extra fields (forbidNonWhitelisted)', async () => {\n115\t      const res = await request(httpServer())\n116\t        .post('/auth/forgot-password')\n117\t        .send({ email: 'user@example.com', maliciousField: 'hacked' });\n118\t\n119\t      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST);\n120\t    });\n121\t  });\n122\t\n123\t  // ========================================================================\n124\t  // 2. Email Enumeration Prevention\n125\t  // ========================================================================\n126\t  describe('Email enumeration prevention', () => {\n127\t    it('should return success even for non-existent email', async () => {\n128\t      const res = await request(httpServer())\n129\t        .post('/auth/forgot-password')\n130\t        .send({ email: 'nonexistent@example.com' });\n131\t\n132\t      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.OK);\n133\t      expect(responseBody(res).success).toBe(true);\n134\t      expect(responseBody(res).message).toContain('If an account');\n135\t    });\n136\t\n137\t    it('should return same response shape for existing email', async () => {\n138\t      const res = await request(httpServer())\n139\t        .post('/auth/forgot-password')\n140\t        .send({ email: 'exists@example.com' });\n141\t\n142\t      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.OK);\n143\t      expect(responseBody(res).success).toBe(true);\n144\t      expect(responseBody(res).message).toContain('If an account');\n145\t    });\n146\t\n147\t    it('should return success even on internal error', async () => {\n148\t      mockAuthNatsClient.send.mockReturnValueOnce(throwError(() => new Error('NATS connection failed')));\n149\t\n150\t      const res = await request(httpServer())\n151\t        .post('/auth/forgot-password')\n152\t        .send({ email: 'user@example.com' });\n153\t\n154\t      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.OK);\n155\t      expect(responseBody(res).success).toBe(true);\n156\t    });\n157\t  });\n158\t\n159\t  // ========================================================================\n160\t  // 3. Token Security\n161\t  // ========================================================================\n162\t  describe('Token security', () => {\n163\t    it('should delegate forgot password to auth-service without local token storage', async () => {\n164\t      await request(httpServer())\n165\t        .post('/auth/forgot-password')\n166\t        .send({ email: 'Test@Test.COM' });\n167\t\n168\t      expect(mockAuthNatsClient.send).toHaveBeenCalledWith(\n169\t        AUTH_PUBLIC_COMMAND_SUBJECTS.REQUEST_PASSWORD_RESET,\n170\t        expect.objectContaining({ email: 'test@test.com' }),\n171\t      );\n172\t    });\n173\t\n174\t    it('should not send raw reset URL material from admin-api', async () => {\n175\t      await request(httpServer())\n176\t        .post('/auth/forgot-password')\n177\t        .send({ email: 'test@test.com' });\n178\t\n179\t      const command = authCommandAt(0);\n180\t\n181\t      expect(command).not.toHaveProperty('rawToken');\n182\t      expect(command).not.toHaveProperty('resetLink');\n183\t      expect(command).not.toHaveProperty('tokenHash');\n184\t    });\n185\t  });\n186\t\n187\t  // ========================================================================\n188\t  // 4. Reset Password - Validation\n189\t  // ========================================================================\n190\t  describe('POST /auth/reset-password - Validation', () => {\n191\t    it('should reject missing token', async () => {\n192\t      const res = await request(httpServer())\n193\t        .post('/auth/reset-password')\n194\t        .send({ newPassword: 'ValidPass123!' });\n195\t\n196\t      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST);\n197\t    });\n198\t\n199\t    it('should reject missing password', async () => {\n200\t      const res = await request(httpServer())\n201\t        .post('/auth/reset-password')\n202\t        .send({ token: 'some-token' });\n203\t\n204\t      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST);\n205\t    });\n206\t\n207\t    it('should reject password shorter than 8 characters', async () => {\n208\t      const res = await request(httpServer())\n209\t        .post('/auth/reset-password')\n210\t        .send({ token: 'some-token', newPassword: 'short' });\n211\t\n212\t      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST);\n213\t    });\n214\t\n215\t    it('should reject password longer than 128 characters', async () => {\n216\t      const longPassword = 'a'.repeat(129);\n217\t      const res = await request(httpServer())\n218\t        .post('/auth/reset-password')\n219\t        .send({ token: 'some-token', newPassword: longPassword });\n220\t\n221\t      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST);\n222\t    });\n223\t\n224\t    it('should accept valid token and password', async () => {\n225\t      const res = await request(httpServer())\n226\t        .post('/auth/reset-password')\n227\t        .send({ token: 'valid-token-string', newPassword: 'ValidPass123!' });\n228\t\n229\t      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.OK);\n230\t      expect(responseBody(res).success).toBe(true);\n231\t    });\n232\t\n233\t    it('should reject extra fields', async () => {\n234\t      const res = await request(httpServer())\n235\t        .post('/auth/reset-password')\n236\t        .send({ token: 'tok', newPassword: 'ValidPass123!', role: 'SUPER_ADMIN' });\n237\t\n238\t      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST);\n239\t    });\n240\t  });\n241\t\n242\t  // ========================================================================\n243\t  // 5. * Password Reset Controller - Security Tests\n *\n * Tests cover:\n * - DTO validation (email, password)\n * - Email enumeration prevention\n * - Auth-service delegation for reset-token/password state\n * - ThrottlePasswordReset decorator presence\n * - Public decorator presence (bypass auth for these endpoints)\n */\nimport { INestApplication, ValidationPipe, HttpStatus } from '@nestjs/common';\nimport { Test, TestingModule } from '@nestjs/testing';\nimport { AUTH_PUBLIC_COMMAND_SUBJECTS } from '@platform/event-contracts';\nimport { of, throwError } from 'rxjs';\nimport request from 'supertest';\n\nimport { PasswordResetController } from '../password-reset.controller';\n\ndescribe('PasswordResetController Security', () => {\n  let app: INestApplication;\n\n  type TestApp = Parameters<typeof request>[0];\n  type PasswordResetResponseBody = {\n    success?: boolean;\n    message?: string;\n  };\n  type AuthNatsSend = jest.Mock<unknown, [string, unknown]>;\n\n  const mockAuthNatsClient = {\n    send: jest.fn() as AuthNatsSend,\n  };\n\n  function httpServer(): TestApp {\n    return app.getHttpServer() as TestApp;\n  }\n\n  function responseBody(res: request.Response): PasswordResetResponseBody {\n    return res.body as PasswordResetResponseBody;\n  }\n\n  function isRecord(value: unknown): value is Record<string, unknown> {\n    return typeof value === 'object' && value !== null && !Array.isArray(value);\n  }\n\n  function authCommandAt(index: number): Record<string, unknown> {\n    const call = mockAuthNatsClient.send.mock.calls[index];\n    if (!call) {\n      throw new Error(`Expected auth NATS command at call index ${index}`);\n    }\n    const payload = call[1];\n    if (!isRecord(payload)) {\n      throw new Error(`Expected auth NATS command payload at call index ${index}`);\n    }\n    return payload;\n  }\n\n  beforeAll(async () => {\n    const module: TestingModule = await Test.createTestingModule({\n      controllers: [PasswordResetController],\n      providers: [\n        { provide: 'AUTH_NATS_CLIENT', useValue: mockAuthNatsClient },\n      ],\n    }).compile();\n\n    app = module.createNestApplication();\n    app.useGlobalPipes(\n      new ValidationPipe({\n        whitelist: true,\n        forbidNonWhitelisted: true,\n        transform: true,\n      }),\n    );\n    await app.init();\n  });\n\n  afterAll(async () => {\n    await app.close();\n  });\n\n  beforeEach(() => {\n    jest.clearAllMocks();\n    mockAuthNatsClient.send.mockReturnValue(of({ success: true }));\n  });\n\n  // ========================================================================\n  // 1. Forgot Password - Validation\n  // ========================================================================\n  describe('POST /auth/forgot-password - Validation', () => {\n    it('should accept valid email', async () => {\n      const res = await request(httpServer())\n        .post('/auth/forgot-password')\n        .send({ email: 'user@example.com' });\n\n      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.OK);\n      expect(responseBody(res).success).toBe(true);\n    });\n\n    it('should reject missing email', async () => {\n      const res = await request(httpServer())\n        .post('/auth/forgot-password')\n        .send({});\n\n      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST);\n    });\n\n    it('should reject invalid email format', async () => {\n      const res = await request(httpServer())\n        .post('/auth/forgot-password')\n        .send({ email: 'not-an-email' });\n\n      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST);\n    });\n\n    it('should reject extra fields (forbidNonWhitelisted)', async () => {\n      const res = await request(httpServer())\n        .post('/auth/forgot-password')\n        .send({ email: 'user@example.com', maliciousField: 'hacked' });\n\n      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST);\n    });\n  });\n\n  // ========================================================================\n  // 2. Email Enumeration Prevention\n  // ========================================================================\n  describe('Email enumeration prevention', () => {\n    it('should return success even for non-existent email', async () => {\n      const res = await request(httpServer())\n        .post('/auth/forgot-password')\n        .send({ email: 'nonexistent@example.com' });\n\n      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.OK);\n      expect(responseBody(res).success).toBe(true);\n      expect(responseBody(res).message).toContain('If an account');\n    });\n\n    it('should return same response shape for existing email', async () => {\n      const res = await request(httpServer())\n        .post('/auth/forgot-password')\n        .send({ email: 'exists@example.com' });\n\n      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.OK);\n      expect(responseBody(res).success).toBe(true);\n      expect(responseBody(res).message).toContain('If an account');\n    });\n\n    it('should return success even on internal error', async () => {\n      mockAuthNatsClient.send.mockReturnValueOnce(throwError(() => new Error('NATS connection failed')));\n\n      const res = await request(httpServer())\n        .post('/auth/forgot-password')\n        .send({ email: 'user@example.com' });\n\n      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.OK);\n      expect(responseBody(res).success).toBe(true);\n    });\n  });\n\n  // ========================================================================\n  // 3. Token Security\n  // ========================================================================\n  describe('Token security', () => {\n    it('should delegate forgot password to auth-service without local token storage', async () => {\n      await request(httpServer())\n        .post('/auth/forgot-password')\n        .send({ email: 'Test@Test.COM' });\n\n      expect(mockAuthNatsClient.send).toHaveBeenCalledWith(\n        AUTH_PUBLIC_COMMAND_SUBJECTS.REQUEST_PASSWORD_RESET,\n        expect.objectContaining({ email: 'test@test.com' }),\n      );\n    });\n\n    it('should not send raw reset URL material from admin-api', async () => {\n      await request(httpServer())\n        .post('/auth/forgot-password')\n        .send({ email: 'test@test.com' });\n\n      const command = authCommandAt(0);\n\n      expect(command).not.toHaveProperty('rawToken');\n      expect(command).not.toHaveProperty('resetLink');\n      expect(command).not.toHaveProperty('tokenHash');\n    });\n  });\n\n  // ========================================================================\n  // 4. Reset Password - Validation\n  // ========================================================================\n  describe('POST /auth/reset-password - Validation', () => {\n    it('should reject missing token', async () => {\n      const res = await request(httpServer())\n        .post('/auth/reset-password')\n        .send({ newPassword: 'ValidPass123!' });\n\n      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST);\n    });\n\n    it('should reject missing password', async () => {\n      const res = await request(httpServer())\n        .post('/auth/reset-password')\n        .send({ token: 'some-token' });\n\n      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST);\n    });\n\n    it('should reject password shorter than 8 characters', async () => {\n      const res = await request(httpServer())\n        .post('/auth/reset-password')\n        .send({ token: 'some-token', newPassword: 'short' });\n\n      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST);\n    });\n\n    it('should reject password longer than 128 characters', async () => {\n      const longPassword = 'a'.repeat(129);\n      const res = await request(httpServer())\n        .post('/auth/reset-password')\n        .send({ token: 'some-token', newPassword: longPassword });\n\n      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST);\n    });\n\n    it('should accept valid token and password', async () => {\n      const res = await request(httpServer())\n        .post('/auth/reset-password')\n        .send({ token: 'valid-token-string', newPassword: 'ValidPass123!' });\n\n      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.OK);\n      expect(responseBody(res).success).toBe(true);\n    });\n\n    it('should reject extra fields', async () => {\n      const res = await request(httpServer())\n        .post('/auth/reset-password')\n        .send({ token: 'tok', newPassword: 'ValidPass123!', role: 'SUPER_ADMIN' });\n\n      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST);\n    });\n  });\n\n  // ========================================================================\n  // 5. Reset Password - Token Verification\n  // ========================================================================\n  describe('Reset password token verification', () => {\n    it('should reject invalid/expired token', async () => {\n      mockAuthNatsClient.send.mockReturnValueOnce(of({\n        success: false,\n        errorCode: 'INVALID_OR_EXPIRED_TOKEN',\n      }));\n\n      const res = await request(httpServer())\n        .post('/auth/reset-password')\n        .send({ token: 'invalid-token', newPassword: 'NewPass123!' });\n\n      expect(res.status).toBe(HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST);\n      expect(responseBody(res).message).toContain('Invalid or expired');\n    });\n\n    it('should delegate the new password to auth-service for hashing and persistence', async () => {\n      await request(httpServer())\n        .post('/auth/reset-password')\n        .send({ token: 'valid-token', newPassword: 'NewSecurePass123!' });\n\n      expect(mockAuthNatsClient.send).toHaveBeenCalledWith(\n        AUTH_PUBLIC_COMMAND_SUBJECTS.RESET_PASSWORD,\n        expect.objectContaining({\n          token: 'valid-token',\n          newPassword: 'NewSecurePass123!',\n        }),\n      );\n    });\n\n    it('should not issue local auth table update statements', async () => {\n      await request(httpServer())\n        .post('/auth/reset-password')\n        .send({ token: 'valid-token', newPassword: 'NewSecurePass123!' });\n\n      expect(mockAuthNatsClient.send).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);\n    });\n  });\n\n  // ========================================================================\n  // 6. SQL Injection Prevention\n  // ========================================================================\n  describe('SQL injection prevention', () => {\n    it('should reject invalid email before auth-service delegation', async () => {\n      await request(httpServer())\n        .post('/auth/forgot-password')\n        .send({ email: \"test@test.com'; DROP TABLE users;--\" });\n\n      expect(mockAuthNatsClient.send).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\n    });\n  });\n});\n","numLines":296,"startLine":1,"totalLines":296}}}

It read as though a shadow run dispatched a narrower\n1302\t    set of tools, and it never did. Collapsed to the one check that was\n1303\t    actually happening; the no-write behaviour of a shadow cycle lives in\n1304\t    the phase preconditions and in `enforce_profile_for_write`, not here.\n1305\t    \"\"\"\n1306\t    emit_progress(\"tools\", cycle_id=context.cycle_id, phase=\"started\")\n1307\t    decisions: list[Any] = []\n1308\t    pressure_summary: dict[str, Any] = {}\n1309\t    for tool in list_tools(base_dir=context.base_dir):\n1310\t        if tool.get(\"status\") not in (\"ACTIVE\", \"SHADOW\", \"CALIBRATE\"):\n1311\t            continue\n1312\t        payload = dict(tool.get(\"default_input\") or {})\n1313\t        payload.update({\"cycle_id\": context.cycle_id, \"pressure_summary\": pressure_summary})\n1314\t        decisions.append(\n1315\t            run_tool(\n1316\t                str(tool[\"tool_id\"]),\n1317\t                payload,\n1318\t                context.cycle_id,\n1319\t                workspace_root=context.workspace_root,\n1320\t                base_dir=context.base_dir,\n1321\t            ),\n1322\t        )\n1323\t    # v2 runtime contract — prefer the per-cycle run index to avoid an\n1324\t    # O(N) scan over a growing runs.jsonl. The helper falls back to the\n1325\t    # strict runs reader for legacy ledgers.\n1326\t    run_summary: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n1327\t    for run in read_runs_for_cycle(base_dir=context.base_dir, cycle_uid=context.cycle_id):\n1328\t        runner = run.get(\"runner\") if isinstance(run.get(\"runner\"), dict) else {}\n1329\t        artifact_ref = run.get(\"artifact_ref\") if isinstance(run.get(\"artifact_ref\"), dict) else None\n1330\t        run_summary.append(\n1331\t            {\n1332\t                \"tool_id\": run.get(\"tool_id\"),\n1333\t                \"run_id\": run.get(\"run_id\"),\n1334\t                \"status\": run.get(\"status\"),\n1335\t                \"artifact_status\": run.get(\"artifact_status\", \"legacy_inline_or_sample_only\"),\n1336\t                \"artifact_ref\": artifact_ref,\n1337\t                \"artifact_hash\": run.get(\"artifact_hash\"),\n1338\t                \"raw_findings_count\": int(runner.get(\"raw_findings_count\") or 0),\n1339\t                \"raw_observations_count\": int(runner.get(\"raw_observations_count\") or 0),"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"5a4c4f70-f8bc-4bf7-9998-cc5c20f832fa","uuid":"ea038cb4-70d8-48fb-92c0-432271db77b4","timestamp":"2026-09-19T15:24:57.968Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-a4a4db32a408/aria-kernel/aria_kernel/cycle.py","content":"    three values in a different order, so the branch on `shadow_only`\n    decided nothing. It read as though a shadow run dispatched a narrower\n    set of tools, and it never did. Collapsed to the one check that was\n    actually happening; the no-write behaviour of a shadow cycle lives in\n    the phase preconditions and in `enforce_profile_for_write`, not here.\n    \"\"\"\n    emit_progress(\"tools\", cycle_id=context.cycle_id, phase=\"started\")\n    decisions: list[Any] = []\n    pressure_summary: dict[str, Any] = {}\n    for tool in list_tools(base_dir=context.base_dir):\n        if tool.get(\"status\") not in (\"ACTIVE\", \"SHADOW\", \"CALIBRATE\"):\n            continue\n        payload = dict(tool.get(\"default_input\") or {})\n        payload.update({\"cycle_id\": context.cycle_id, \"pressure_summary\": pressure_summary})\n        decisions.append(\n            run_tool(\n                str(tool[\"tool_id\"]),\n                payload,\n                context.cycle_id,\n                workspace_root=context.workspace_root,\n                base_dir=context.base_dir,\n            ),\n        )\n    # v2 runtime contract — prefer the per-cycle run index to avoid an\n    # O(N) scan over a growing runs.jsonl. The helper falls back to the\n    # strict runs reader for legacy ledgers.\n    run_summary: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n    for run in read_runs_for_cycle(base_dir=context.base_dir, cycle_uid=context.cycle_id):\n        runner = run.get(\"runner\") if isinstance(run.get(\"runner\"), dict) else {}\n        artifact_ref = run.get(\"artifact_ref\") if isinstance(run.get(\"artifact_ref\"), dict) else None\n        run_summary.append(\n            {\n                \"tool_id\": run.get(\"tool_id\"),\n                \"run_id\": run.get(\"run_id\"),\n                \"status\": run.get(\"status\"),\n                \"artifact_status\": run.get(\"artifact_status\", \"legacy_inline_or_sample_only\"),\n                \"artifact_ref\": artifact_ref,\n                \"artifact_hash\": run.get(\"artifact_hash\"),\n                \"raw_findings_count\": int(runner.get(\"raw_findings_count\") or 0),\n                \"raw_observations_count\": int(runner.get(\"raw_observations_count\") or 0),","numLines":40,"startLine":1300,"totalLines":3966}}}
